MP #1: One Web Page To Rule Them All
Due: Tuesday, Sep 22, 2026, 11:59PM CT
Table of Contents

1. [Assignment](https://github.com/cs409-fa25/mp1#assignment)
2. [Grading Breakdown](https://github.com/cs409-fa25/mp1#grading-breakdown)
3. [Rules](https://github.com/cs409-fa25/mp1#rules)
4. [Getting Started](https://github.com/cs409-fa25/mp1#getting-started)
5. [Submission Details](https://github.com/cs409-fa25/mp1#submission-details)
6. [Large Language Model Usage Policy](https://github.com/cs409-fa25/mp1#large-language-model-usage-policy)

Assignment
Task
In this programming assignment, you will design and implement a single-page website with specified functionality and formatting using only HTML, SCSS, and Javascript. The content of the website is up to you. For example, the subject can be a company/organization, a product, or a personal/portfolio page. This assignment will give you first-hand experience with HTML5 (e.g. header, canvas, video), CSS3/SASS (e.g. use of webfonts, animations, layout), and Javascript(ES5 or ES6).
Requirements
Your webpage will have to implement the features listed below. Note that the examples are only meant to illustrate the features. You do not have to make your webpage look similar to the examples. You just have to incorporate that feature in some form.

1. Layout: All content must be laid out in a single page with full-width horizontal stripes/sections including a header and a footer - [Example](https://cs409-fa25.github.io/fa-26/images/mp1/2.png)
2. Sticky Navbar: A top navigation bar that sticks to the top of the window when scrolling - [Example](https://cs409-fa25.github.io/fa-26/images/mp1/3.gif)
3. Position Indicator: Indicator of the current reading position visible in the navigation bar (see above gif for example). Make sure your implementation highlights the last menu item when you scroll to the bottom of the page. ( Implementation Tip : To figure out which menu item to highlight, you’ll have to figure out which section lies directly below the bottom margin of the navigation bar. You can use methods that give you the height and position of different divs to figure that out. You will want to recompute this whenever there is a scroll event.)
4. Navbar Resizing: Navigation bar must be larger when users are at the top of the page. As users start scrolling down, it should resize and become smaller. Note that the text font size should also start larger and become smaller as users scroll down - [Example](https://cs409-fa25.github.io/fa-26/images/mp1/4.gif)
5. Smooth Scrolling: Smooth scrolling when navigating to a different section using the navigation bar - [Example](https://cs409-fa25.github.io/fa-26/images/mp1/5.gif)
6. Carousel: A section with a carousel/slider using a minimum of three slides. It should include the navigation arrows on the side - [Example](https://cs409-fa25.github.io/fa-26/images/mp1/6.gif)
7. Multi-column layout: A section with Multi-column content (3 or more columns) - [Example](https://cs409-fa25.github.io/fa-26/images/mp1/8.png)
8. Centering: Content in each horizontal stripe/section must be horizontally centered. At least one element on the page must be vertically centered. The vertically-centered element should remain centered even if the dimensions of its outer element changes.
9. Responsiveness: Your webpage should look good when resized to the following resolutions:

* 1920x1080
* 1366x768
* 1280x720
* 1024x768
* 768x1024

10. Background Image: A section which uses a fixed-position background image - [Example](https://cs409-fa25.github.io/fa-26/images/mp1/9.gif)
11. Modal: A section which uses modal windows with additional content - [Example](https://cs409-fa25.github.io/fa-26/images/mp1/10.gif)
12. Video: Embedded video using HTML5 video tag - [Example](https://cs409-fa25.github.io/fa-26/images/mp1/11_2.gif)
13. SCSS features/CSS variables - You must utilize some SCSS features (e.g. variables, mixins, etc). A plain CSS file will receive less points.
14. CSS3 Animations: At least one use of CSS3 animations (e.g. fade in/out, transitions)
15. At least one use of scalable vector icons through CSS (e.g. FontAwesome)
16. Inclusion of social media icons

Grading Breakdown

* Layout and Overall Design (includes but not limited to the use of consistent paddings and margins, appropriate font sizes and weights, centering and uniformity, foreground and background colors, user experience, efforts to make the UI better, etc.) - 20%
* Code (follows HTML5, SASS/SCSS, ES5 or ES6 best practices) - 12%
* Smooth Scrolling - 10%
* Carousel - 10%
* Modal - 10%
* Responsiveness - 10%
* Position Indicator - 5%
* Navbar Resizing - 5%
* Multi-column layout - 5%
* SCSS features/CSS variables - 1%
* CSS3 Animations - 5%
* Centering - 2%
* Video - 2%
* Sticky Navbar - 1%
* Background Image - 1%
* Scalable vector icons and inclusion of social media icons - 1%

Rules

1. This is an individual assignment. No collaboration is permitted.
2. It is not permitted to copy/paste code that is not your own. You are, however, free to look at different code sources for inspiration and clarity. All sources (code as well as reading material) that you reference to complete this assignment must be declared in the submission.
3. No libraries are permitted (i.e. Bootstrap, JQuery, React, UIKit...etc.).
4. There should be no use of inline styling.
5. No inline script tags should be used.
6. HTML tables cannot be used for layout.
7. If you think something you’re doing might not be acceptable, please ask on Piazza.

Getting Started
Create Your Repo From the Template

1. On the [class repo](https://github.com/cs409-fa25/mp1), click Use this template > Create a new repository. Name it `mp1` and make it public (GitHub Pages requires a public repo on a free account).
2. Clone the repository you just created: `git clone git@github.com:<your-github-username>/mp1.git mp1`, then `cd mp1`
3. Install dependencies: `npm install`
4. Start the dev server: `npm start`
5. Open a browser and go to `http://localhost:8080/` to view your page. You should see "Hello World! Welcome to MP1!" at the top of the screen. Note that if for some reason your port 8080 is occupied, it will default to 8081.
6. Work on your MP code and satisfy all the grading [requirements](https://github.com/cs409-fa25/mp1#Requirements)

Deploy your code
In order for us to view and grade your MP, you will be deploying your webpage with GitHub's pipelines. This should happen automatically after pushing to your repository, through Github Actions deployment pipeline.
You will need to do some additional work to properly deploy the website. There are a few settings you will need to change in your GitHub repository.

1. Set GitHub Pages Deployment Source to Github Actions
   * In your Github repo, go to Settings > Pages > Build and Deployment > Source > Select "GitHub Actions" `.github/workflows/static.yml` file automatically makes a GitHub CI pipeline run to build and deploy your code as a website. After the pipeline finishes, your site should be live at `https://<your-github-username>.github.io/mp1`. It may take up to 10-30 minutes for the site to go live after the first deployment.

Submission Details

1. Make a video (3 minutes max) demo-ing your deployed website and upload it to Google Drive. Share it with `uiuc.web.programming@gmail.com` and put the share link in the submission form.

* Show the url to prove you are on your deployed website. Then show all the requirement features you fulfilled in your mp.
* If you were unable to deploy your website, you can demo your mp locally for some point deduction (hard capped at 80%)
   * Just make sure you do `git status` and `git log` first so we can see your last edits.

2. Fill out and submit the form [here](https://forms.gle/jfgQnaTSVmhrt2DH8)

Large Language Model Usage Policy
We acknowledge the transformative potential of Large Language Models (LLM) in generating code; however, we are still in the nascent stages of understanding how to embed LLMs in developer workflows to write code more efficiently while maintaining quality. Therefore, we will not be teaching students directly how to use LLMs to develop web applications.
As part of this class, we do encourage students to experiment with LLM services such as OpenAI's ChatGPT to generate source code for MPs. If LLMs are used to generate code for an MP, students must (1) submit their chatlogs along with their source code, and (2) answer survey questions related to their experience using LLMs in the grading form. Failure to do this will be a violation of the academic integrity policy of this course.

## Other topics

The page structure stays the same whatever you choose; only the text and media change. Pick something you already have photos or video for, since media is the slowest part to gather.

| Topic | Carousel | 3 columns | Modals | Video | Fixed background |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Personal portfolio** (current plan) | Projects | Skills | Job or internship details | Project demo | Workspace or campus photo |
| **Coffee shop or café** | Signature drinks | Menu: coffee, tea, pastries | Full recipe or nutrition per item | Barista pouring latte art | Café interior |
| **Travel blog** | Destinations | Plan, pack, explore | Trip itineraries | Drone clip | Landscape |
| **Student club or org** (e.g. ACM, a sports club) | Past events | Mission, members, sponsors | Officer bios or event details | Event highlights | Group photo |
| **Band or musician** | Albums | Tour dates, merch, press | Lyrics or track lists | Music video or live clip | Stage photo |
| **Fictional startup or product** (app or gadget) | Features | Pricing tiers | Feature deep-dives or FAQ | Product promo | Hero product shot |
| **Fitness or gym** | Class types | Programs: strength, cardio, yoga | Trainer profiles | Workout clip | Gym interior |
| **Restaurant or recipe site** | Dishes | Starters, mains, desserts | Full recipes | Cooking clip | Kitchen or table spread |
| **Game or movie fan page** | Characters | Story, gameplay, lore | Character bios | Trailer | Key art |
| **National park or city guide** | Landmarks | Eat, stay, see | Landmark details and hours | Scenic clip | Skyline or vista |

My recommendations:
- **Personal portfolio:** you can reuse it for internship applications, and you can write the content from memory.
- **Café or fictional startup:** these tend to look the most polished, which helps the 20% design score, because product and food photos from Unsplash or Pexels are free and look great with a fixed background.

Free video for the video section is available on Pexels Videos and Pixabay. Whatever media you use, credit it in the README's Sources section.

Pick a topic and I'll update the plan's sample text and colour palette to match.

The plan is already written for a personal portfolio, so nothing in it needs to change.

The next step is Chunk 0: creating your repo from the course template. It's a public repo on your GitHub account, so either you do it or you tell me to. There are two ways:

- **Yourself:** on github.com, go to the template and click **Use this template → Create a new repository**. Name it `mp1` and make it Public. Then clone it into this folder:
  ```bash
  git clone git@github.com:<your-github-username>/mp1.git mp1
  ```
- **Me:** if the GitHub CLI is set up, I can run this, which creates the repo and clones it in one step:
  ```bash
  gh repo create mp1 --template cs409-fa25/mp1 --public --clone
  ```
  Then I'd run `npm install` and `npm start`. I won't commit or push anything.

Either way, switching Pages to "GitHub Actions" is a settings change you need to make yourself, and so is the first push.

While that's happening, send me the content for the page. I'll use placeholders for anything you skip:

1. **Name and tagline** for the hero (e.g. "Darshan Hene · CS @ UIUC · Full-stack developer").
2. **About:** three short themes for the 3 columns, like Develop / Design / Ship, or your own, such as languages, interests, currently learning.
3. **Projects** for the carousel: 3 or more, each with a title, one-line description and a screenshot if you have one.
4. **Experience** for the modal cards: 3 roles, each with dates and 3–4 bullet points.
5. **Video:** a demo of one of your projects, or a stock clip from Pexels.
6. **Social links:** GitHub, LinkedIn, and any others you want in the footer.
7. **Colour preference:** the plan uses dark with an orange accent. Say if you'd like something else.

----- CLAUDE (2026-09-22 23:54:57) -----
Yes, that's expected. The course folder you made is `MP1/`, and the clone went into a subfolder inside it called `mp1/`. macOS ignores capitalisation in folder names, so it looks like `mp1` inside `mp1`:

```
MP1/                        ← your course folder (not the GitHub repo)
├── docs/superpowers/plans/ ← the plan (stays out of the repo)
├── .claude/launch.json     ← dev-server config (stays out of the repo)
├── .gitignore, .git/, package-lock.json   ← leftovers, not needed
└── mp1/                    ← THE repo: github.com/darshandhene/mp1
    ├── src/ …
    └── package.json …
```

Only the inner `mp1/` is your GitHub repo and the thing that gets deployed. Run all git and npm commands from inside it.

The outer folder also has its own empty `.git` and a stray `package-lock.json`. Neither is needed, and the outer `.git` makes it easy to commit into the wrong repo by accident. I'd delete both, but it's your call. I won't delete them unless you ask.

## Where the media goes

- **Hero image:** `mp1/src/assets/images/hero.jpg`, about 1920px wide and ideally under 500 KB.
- **Video:** `mp1/src/assets/video/intro.mp4`, a short clip under about 10 MB.
- **Optional:** `mp1/src/assets/images/video-poster.jpg`, the still frame shown before the video plays.

Create the `images/` and `video/` folders if they don't exist. Webpack copies everything in `src/assets/` into the build.

    #!/usr/bin/env bash
    # Resolve and ensure the working-tree directory SDD uses for one plan's
    # short-lived artifacts: task briefs, implementer reports, review packages,
    # and the progress ledger. Print the plan directory's absolute path.
    #
    # One directory per plan (.superpowers/sdd/<plan-basename>/) so a follow-up
    # plan in the same working tree can never read or overwrite another plan's
    # artifacts. A stale ledger misread as current progress makes controllers
    # skip whole task sequences — plan-scoping removes that failure structurally.
    #
    # The workspace lives in the working tree (not under .git/) because Claude Code
    # treats .git/ as a protected path and denies agent writes there — which blocks
    # an implementer subagent from writing its report file. A self-ignoring
    # .gitignore at .superpowers/sdd/ keeps every plan's workspace out of
    # `git status` and out of accidental commits without modifying any tracked file.
    #
    # Single source of truth for the workspace location, so task-brief and
    # review-package cannot drift to different directories.
    #
    # Usage: sdd-workspace PLAN_FILE
    set -euo pipefail
    
    if [ $# -ne 1 ]; then
      echo

### Spec Compliance

**src/css/_carousel.scss**
- Matches ruling 1: `.carousel__icon` and `.carousel__subtitle` are styled, no `img` rule present (correctly dropped vs. the brief's stub which had `img { ... }`).
- Matches ruling 2: slide padding `3rem 4.5rem` desktop, stepped down at `respond-to(tablet)` (2.5rem 3rem) and `respond-to(mobile)` (2rem 1.5rem); uses `$color-surface` and `$radius` (line 26, 37-38). Improves on the brief's stub, which hardcoded `12px` for `&amp;__viewport { border-radius: 12px; }` — implementer correctly used `$radius` instead (line 25).
- Matches ruling 3: arrows at `-24px` desktop, `8px` inside `respond-to(tablet)` (lines 98-113) — no separate mobile override, which is fine since tablet's rule cascades down to mobile (`respond-to` uses `max-width`, so mobile also matches tablet's query).
- ⚠️ Centering claim ("vertically centered on the card, not on card+dots") is only approximated — see Issues (Important) below; not fully correct by my calculation.
- Matches ruling 6 (SCSS variables/mixins reuse): uses `$color-surface`, `$color-muted`, `$color-accent`, `$color-bg`, `$color-text`, `$radius`, `respond-to`, `flex-center` — all confirmed present in `_variables.scss`/`_mixins.scss`. No unused/undefined tokens.
- Nothing extra beyond what's needed; `.tags` override is a single `margin-top`, correctly deferring to the base `.tags` rule in `_base.scss:84` rather than duplicating it.

**src/js/carousel.js**
- Matches ruling 4: `ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight` listener is attached to `root` (carousel container), not `document` (line 190 in diff / carousel.js), so it only fires via bubbling when focus is inside the carousel. Correct.
- Matches ruling 3: only `track.style.transform` is set from JS (line 175). No other inline styles.
- Matches ruling 5: active dot gets both `carousel__dot--active` class and `aria-current="true"`, removed from others (lines 176-184) — this is an improvement over the brief's stub, which only toggled the class.
- `initCarousel(root)` signature matches brief exactly.
- No autoplay, no swipe — confirmed, nothing beyond arrows/dots/keyboard.
- ⚠️ No `preventDefault()` on the arrow-key handler — brief doesn't explicitly require it, so not a compliance miss, but worth noting under code quality.

- Matches ruling exactly: `import { initCarousel } from './carousel.js'` added, `initCarousel(document.getElementById('carousel'))` called immediately after `initNav()` inside the existing `DOMContentLoaded` listener (diff lines 142-148). No leftover or misordered code.

HTML was untouched, as claimed and as required.

### Strengths
- Correctly diverged from the brief's stub in two places where the ruling supersedes it: dropped the `img` rule (no images in markup) and used `$radius` instead of a hardcoded `12px`.
- `aria-current="true"` addition on the active dot is a genuine accessibility improvement beyond the brief's minimum.
- Keyboard handling correctly scoped to the carousel root via bubbling rather than a document-level listener — satisfies ruling 4 precisely.
- Clean reuse of `.tags` base styling; no duplicated CSS.
- Wrap-around index math `(i + slides.length) % slides.length` is correct for single-step increments/decrements from any valid `index` (verified below).

### Issues

#### Important
- **`src/css/_carousel.scss:79-81`** — Arrow vertical centering overshoots. `top: calc(50% - 1.75rem)` plus `transform: translateY(-50%)` is meant to shift the arrow's center up from the midpoint of `.carousel` (card + dots) to the midpoint of the card alone, per ruling 2. The needed shift is half the height added by the dots block below the viewport: dots row (~10px, from `.carousel__dot` width/height) + `margin-top: 1rem` (16px) ≈ 26px total, so the correct offset is ≈13px (0.8125rem). The implementation uses 28px (1.75rem) — more than double what's needed — so the arrows will sit visibly too high, roughly 15px above the true vertical center of the card, at every breakpoint (this rule isn't inside any `respond-to` block, so it applies universally). This directly fails the specific ruling that arrows be "vertically centered on the card, not on card+dots."

#### Minor
- **`src/js/carousel.js` (arrow-key handler, ~lines 190-193)** — No `event.preventDefault()` on `ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight`. Not required by the brief, but if focus ever lands on a scrollable ancestor this could double up with native scroll behavior. Low risk since focus stays on buttons, but cheap to add for robustness.
- **`src/css/_carousel.scss:78-114`** — `.carousel__arrow` buttons don't use the `flex-center` mixin (or any explicit `display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center`) to center the Font Awesome chevron icon inside the 48×48 circle. It's likely close enough visually given default button text-align/UA styling, but this is inconsistent with `.carousel__dots` (which does use `flex-center`) and leaves icon centering to browser defaults rather than explicit CSS — a DRY/consistency nit rather than a functional bug.
- Report's verification section (task-8-report.md) explicitly states no live-DOM/browser check was performed — the vertical-centering issue above is exactly the kind of defect a quick visual check would have caught. Not a spec miss per se, but the "Verification" claim of confidence is weaker than it reads.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Needs fixes

**Reasoning:** All three files are otherwise faithful to the brief and the controller rulings — markup/selectors line up exactly, the JS logic (wrap-around math, dot sync, `aria-current`, keyboard scoping, no autoplay/swipe) is correct and clean, and `main.js` wiring is exact. However, ruling 2 explicitly calls for arrows "vertically centered on the card, not on card+dots," and the chosen offset (`1.75rem`) overshoots the correct value (~`0.8125rem`) by roughly 2x, so the arrows will be visibly misaligned relative to the card at every resolution. That's a concrete, calculable defect against an explicit ruling, not a matter of taste, so this task can't be trusted as complete until the offset is corrected (or centering is redone, e.g. by wrapping viewport+arrows in their own positioning container so `top: 50%` needs no manual compensation at all).</result>

<result>### Spec Compliance (✅ | ❌ with file:line; ⚠️ cannot verify)

- ✅ Work grid 3 columns at desktop and 768px, collapses to 1 column only at `respond-to(small)` (≤600px) — `src/css/_modal.scss:18-20`, confirmed `small: 600px` in `src/css/_variables.scss:22`, and `mobile: 768px` at `_variables.scss:21` is correctly *not* used as the collapse trigger (Ruling 1).
- ✅ Focus on open goes to `.modal__close` (`src/js/modal.js:14`); focus returns to the triggering card on close (`src/js/modal.js:13,20,30`); Esc closes (`:56-58`); backdrop click closes (`:44-46`); click on dialog contents does not (target check is on `modal` itself, dialog clicks don't match) (Ruling 2).
- ✅ `transitionend` hide guarded by `event.target === modal &amp;&amp; !modal.classList.contains('modal--open')` — `src/js/modal.js:37-41` (Ruling 3).
- ✅ `body.no-scroll { overflow: hidden; }` — `src/css/_modal.scss:141-143`; modal `z-index: 200` (`_modal.scss:63`) above navbar `z-index: 100` (`src/css/_nav.scss:8`) (Ruling 4).
- ✅ `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` disables `.modal`/`.modal__dialog` transitions (`_modal.scss:134-139`); hide path without `transitionend` is handled synchronously in `closeDialog()` via `prefersReducedMotion()` check (`src/js/modal.js:28`) (Ruling 5).
- ✅ Markup untouched; selectors (`work__grid`, `work__card`, `work__role`, `work__dates`, `modal`, `modal__dialog`, `modal__close`, `modal__meta`, `modal__list`) match `src/index.html` exactly (spot-checked lines 148-297).
- ✅ Dialog `h3` gets `padding-right` clearance from the close button via `$modal-title-padding-right` — `_modal.scss:6-9, 96-99`.
- ✅ `main.js` imports and calls `initModals()` after `initCarousel()` inside the existing `DOMContentLoaded` listener — `src/js/main.js:3,8`.
- ✅ No `style=""`, `&lt;style&gt;`, inline `&lt;script&gt;`, or `&lt;table&gt;`; SCSS reuses `$radius`, `$color-*`, `flex-center`, `card` mixin, and the `_carousel.scss`-style pattern of naming shared geometry as variables instead of magic numbers.
- ✅ At least one CSS transition present (opacity + transform fades).
- ⚠️ Visual correctness at the five graded resolutions (1920x1080 … 1024x768) — not independently re-rendered; report's manual checks at 1280/768/600 are plausible given the CSS but not verified by me.

### Strengths

- Single `closeDialog()`/`openDialog()` pair is the only path in and out of a modal — no duplicated close logic across the close button, backdrop, and Escape handlers.
- The `transitionend` guard and reduced-motion synchronous hide are exactly the two things the brief's naive sample code (`Step 2` in the brief) got wrong, and both are correctly fixed here.
- Naming shared close-button geometry (`$modal-close-inset`, `$modal-close-size`, `$modal-close-clearance`) as derived SCSS variables instead of duplicating magic numbers between `.modal__close` and the `h3` padding is good practice and matches the `_carousel.scss` convention cited in the brief.

### Issues

#### Important

- **No protection against a second modal opening while one is already open (`src/js/modal.js:7-15`).** `openDialog()` unconditionally does `modal.hidden = false; ...; openModal = modal;` with no check for an existing `openModal`. Because all `.modal` elements sit as later siblings after the six `.work__card` triggers in the DOM (`src/index.html:148-178` vs `226+`) and `hidden` modals are removed from tab order while an *open* modal is not focus-trapped, Shift+Tab from the open modal's `.modal__close` (its only focusable descendant) lands back on a preceding `.work__card` trigger button while the current modal is still open and visible. Pressing Enter there calls `openDialog()` again: the old modal is left `hidden=false`/`modal--open` with no live reference (orphaned — its own backdrop-click listener still fires `closeDialog()`, but that function now only acts on the new `openModal`, so clicking the old modal's backdrop silently closes the *new* modal instead of itself), while `openModal`/`lastTrigger` now point only at the second modal. Net effect: two full-screen modals stacked, keyboard/backdrop control desynced from what's visually on top, and no way to close the orphaned one through documented ×/Esc/backdrop paths. This is the exact "opening a second modal while one is open" case the review was scoped to check, and it is reachable via keyboard alone (no exotic script needed).
- **Same-tick open→close race leaves a permanently stuck, unresponsive modal (`src/js/modal.js:7-15` interacting with `:17-31`).** `openDialog()` schedules `requestAnimationFrame(() =&gt; modal.classList.add('modal--open'))` but never cancels it. If `closeDialog()` runs before that rAF callback fires (e.g., Escape pressed in the same frame the modal opened, plausible for a keyboard user who activates a trigger with Enter and taps Escape immediately after, or via any near-simultaneous input), the sequence is: open() sets `hidden=false`, schedules the class-add, sets `openModal`; close() then runs, sets `openModal = null`, removes `no-scroll`, removes `modal--open` (currently absent — no-op); the previously queued rAF then fires and re-adds `modal--open`, making the modal fade back in even though the app believes nothing is open. Because `openModal` is now `null`, every subsequent close attempt (Esc, backdrop click, even a fresh `.modal__close` click if reachable) hits the `if (!openModal) return;` guard in `closeDialog()` (`:18`) and no-ops — the modal is stuck open with no code path left to hide it, and `no-scroll` has already been removed so the page behind it scrolls while a full-viewport modal sits inert on top. This directly matches the "re-open during fade-out" case flagged for review.

#### Minor

- No focus trap inside the open modal (Tab/Shift+Tab can leave the dialog to background content). Not explicitly required by Ruling 2 (which only specifies focus-on-open and focus-return-on-close), but it's the root cause enabling the Important double-open finding above; worth adding `Tab`/`Shift+Tab` wrapping within `.modal__dialog` while `openModal` is set.
- `modal.querySelector('.modal__close')` is called twice per modal (once in `openDialog` at `:14`, once in `initModals` at `:43`) — trivial, but could be cached per modal in a `Map` alongside the guard work.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Needs fixes

**Reasoning:** The rulings that were the explicit focus of this task (grid breakpoints, transitionend guard, reduced-motion hide, z-index/no-scroll, focus-on-open/return-on-close) are all correctly implemented and verifiable in the diff. However, the two open/close state-machine bugs above are real, reachable (one via ordinary keyboard navigation, the other via a same-frame open/close race) and match precisely the failure modes this review was scoped to check ("opening a second modal while one is open" and "re-open during fade-out"). Both stem from the same root cause: `openDialog`/`closeDialog` operate on a single shared `openModal`/`lastTrigger` pair with no guard against re-entrancy and no cancellation of the pending rAF class-add. These can leave the UI in an unrecoverable stuck-open or cross-modal-close state, which is a correctness gap serious enough to block approval until `openDialog` refuses/handles the already-open case (e.g., close the previous modal first, or ignore triggers while `openModal` is set) and the rAF callback is guarded or cancelled if a close happens first.</result>

- **768px layout:** the About and Experience grids keep 3 columns at 768×1024, because that's a graded size and multi-column layout is graded. The cost is slightly narrow cards at that width.

- **Modals:** opening a second modal instantly closes the first. There's no focus trap inside the dialog; I deferred that as minor.

- The page isn't built for widths around 440px: at that size the nav links overflow and "Contact" gets cut off. It's not a graded size, but I'll handle it in the Chunk 5 responsive pass.

# AI Usage Disclosure

- OpenAI Codex

* Keep the entire relevant conversation, including your prompts—not only the AI’s final answers.

* Do not use inline CSS or inline script tags.
* Make sure you understand and can explain the submitted code during your video.

## Rulings for ambiguities (decided — follow them)
1. Video file: `src/assets/video/16489854_1920_1080_30fps.mp4` (already exists, 14.5 MB). There is NO poster image — omit the `poster` attribute. Replace the HTML comment inside `.video__wrapper` in `src/index.html` with:
   `<video controls preload="metadata" playsinline>` + `<source src="assets/video/16489854_1920_1080_30fps.mp4" type="video/mp4">` + fallback text "Your browser does not support the video tag." Keep the 4-space-per-level indentation used in index.html. Add a short intro `<p class="video__caption">` is NOT wanted — don't add extra content.
2. `html-loader` resolves `<source src>` through webpack, and `webpack.config.js` has no rule for mp4, so the build will fail. In `webpack.config.js` change the asset rule's test regex from `/\.(eot|svg|ttf|woff|woff2|png|jpg|gif)$/i` to `/\.(eot|svg|ttf|woff|woff2|png|jpg|gif|mp4|webm)$/i`. Change nothing else in that file.
3. At the mobile breakpoint (≤768px) the About grid may drop to 1 column as the brief says. Do NOT do that at 1024px.

### Integration & quality issues

#### Critical
1. **The chat log is missing.** `llm_logs.csv:1` and `AI_USAGE.md` both link to `chatlogs/claude-code-session.txt`, which is not in the folder. This is a hard submission rule. Export the session(s) into that file, or replace the CSV line with real share links.
2. **The live site is out of date.** All the Task 12/13 work is uncommitted: `git status` shows 8 modified files, and `AI_USAGE.md` and `chatlogs/` are untracked. The Pages workflow (`.github/workflows/static.yml`) only builds on push to `main`, so the graded URL still serves f80f570. After committing and pushing:
   - confirm the Actions run succeeded;
   - check that `hero.jpg` and the video load under `/mp1/` on the live URL.
   
   The asset paths are relative (`url('../assets/images/hero.jpg')` in `_hero.scss:16`, `assets/video/...` in `index.html:199`) and resolved by webpack's default `publicPath: auto`, so they should work. The controller verified the dev server, but nobody has checked the deployed site yet.

#### Important
3. **Invalid HTML in the work cards** (`index.html:150-152` and the 5 cards after it). A `<button>` may only contain phrasing content, so the W3C validator flags each `<h3>` and `<p>` inside one. A grader checking code quality could plausibly run the validator.
   - Fix (about 10 minutes): change them to `<span class="work__title">`, `<span class="work__role">` and `<span class="work__dates">`, all `display: block`.
   - Move the `.work__card h3` rules (`_modal.scss:51-53`) to `.work__title`, adding `font-family: $font-heading; font-size: 1.17rem; font-weight: 600`.
4. **Leftover TODO in the README** (`<!-- TODO(user): confirm the video source -->`). Confirm the Pexels ID and credit, then delete the comment.

#### Minor
5. The social icons' hover lift does nothing (`_footer.scss:35-42`). The `a` is an inline element, so `transform` is ignored. Adding `display: inline-block` fixes it with one line.
6. Dead code:
   - `src/assets/image.jpg` is an unused template file, and CopyPlugin still copies it into the build.
   - `--accent` and `--nav-height` in `:root` (`_variables.scss:26-29`) are never used.
   - `NAV_SMALL = 60` in `nav.js:2` duplicates `$nav-height-small` by hand.
7. Partial naming doesn't match content: `.work` styles live in `_modal.scss` and `.contact` in `_footer.scss`. The README documents this, so it can stay.
8. Smooth scroll has two weaknesses (`nav.js:33-46`):
   - It never cancels a running animation, so two quick nav clicks start two rAF loops that fight each other until the second one wins.
   - It ignores `prefers-reduced-motion`, while the hero and modal respect it.
   
   It could also keep scrolling behind a modal opened mid-scroll, which is harmless.
9. `README.md` says "production bundle", but `webpack.config.js:5` builds in `mode: "development"`. That's the template default, so don't change the config; just fix the wording.
10. Checked and found clean: stacking order (carousel arrows z 2 < navbar 100 < modal 200); `.navbar .nav-link` styles don't leak onto the hero `.btn`; the indicator only looks at `.navbar__links`, so the brand link and hero button are never highlighted; filenames are all lowercase, so Linux CI has no case problems; the 14.5 MB video is emitted twice (about 29 MB), well under the Pages limits.

Reading the diff and report:

**Finding 1: Arrow vertical centering overshot**

Line 87 in the new _carousel.scss:
```scss
top: calc(50% - (#{$carousel-dots-gap} + #{$carousel-dot-size}) / 2);
```

With variables defined at lines 13-14:
```scss
$carousel-dots-gap: 1rem;    // 16px
$carousel-dot-size: 10px;    // 10px
```

Calculation: `(16px + 10px) / 2 = 13px` (vs. old magic `1.75rem = 28px`, an overshoot of 15px matching the controller's measurement).

The dots row height components are now properly sourced: `$carousel-dots-gap` reused at line 126 (`.carousel__dots { margin-top }`), `$carousel-dot-size` reused at lines 130–131 (`.carousel__dot { width/height }`). Offset is derived, not a literal.

**Status: ADDRESSED** — file: src/css/_carousel.scss:87, 13–14, 126, 130–131

---

**Finding 2: Arrow overlap with slide text at ≤1024px**

Lines 15–18 define derived variables:
```scss
$carousel-arrow-size: 48px;
$carousel-arrow-inset-tablet: 8px;
$carousel-arrow-clearance: 12px;
$carousel-slide-padding-x-inset: $carousel-arrow-inset-tablet + $carousel-arrow-size + $carousel-arrow-clearance;
```

Calculation: `8 + 48 + 12 = 68px` (meeting the ≥~68px requirement).

Lines 46–52 apply it to both tablet and mobile:
```scss
@include respond-to(tablet) {
  padding: 2.5rem $carousel-slide-padding-x-inset;
}
@include respond-to(mobile) {
  padding: 2rem $carousel-slide-padding-x-inset;
}
```

The report correctly reasons why mobile reuses tablet's horizontal padding: `respond-to(tablet)` (max-width 1024px) encompasses `respond-to(mobile)` (max-width 768px), and arrows stay inset at 8px at both breakpoints.

Controller measurements confirm: arrow right edge 118px (1024) / 94px (768) vs. text left edge 130px / 106px = 12px clearance maintained, no overflow.

The extra branch (`fadeInWasPending` at `modal.js:47`, used at `modal.js:55`) is correct and necessary: it's captured *before* the cancel/null-out, so it accurately reflects whether the modal had ever become visible; when true, `modal--open` was never applied, so removing it is a no-op and no `transitionend` will ever fire, making the synchronous `hidden = true` required (mirrors the existing reduced-motion synchronous-hide case at the same line via `||`).

  - Focus on open → close button: `modal.js:34` (also runs for the newly-opened modal in the switch case; `hideImmediately` correctly does *not* touch focus for the orphaned modal).
  - Focus return to opening card on normal close: `modal.js:57`, unaffected by the fix.

  - Reduced-motion synchronous hide: preserved via the `||` at `modal.js:55`.
  - `body.no-scroll`: added once in `openDialog` (`modal.js:31`) and removed only in `closeDialog` (`modal.js:53`). In the switch-modal path, `hideImmediately` intentionally leaves `no-scroll` alone and `openDialog`'s `add()` is idempotent, so scroll stays locked continuously across the switch rather than flickering unlocked — correct, not a regression.
  - Single global `pendingFrame` is safe because only one modal is ever mid-open-transition at a time: the switch path cancels/clears any old pending frame synchronously before scheduling the new one (`modal.js:23` runs before `modal.js:27`).

## What Was Requested
Task brief: /Users/darshan/Documents/Fall 2026/CS-409-Art of web programming/MP1/mp1/.superpowers/sdd/2026-09-22-mp1-single-page-site/task-9-brief.md

Controller rulings that amend the brief (binding):
1. Work grid: 3 columns on desktop AND at 768px; collapse to 1 column only at `respond-to(small)` (≤600px), not `mobile`.
2. Focus: on open focus the close button; on close return focus to the card that opened it. Esc closes; backdrop click closes; clicks inside the dialog don't.
3. The `transitionend` hide must be guarded: only hide if the event target is the modal itself AND the modal is not currently open (so child transitions or a fast re-open can't hide an open modal).

----- CLAUDE (2026-09-23 00:32:29) -----
### Spec Compliance (✅ | ❌ with file:line; ⚠️ cannot verify)

- ✅ Work grid 3 columns at desktop and 768px, collapses to 1 column only at `respond-to(small)` (≤600px) — `src/css/_modal.scss:18-20`, confirmed `small: 600px` in `src/css/_variables.scss:22`, and `mobile: 768px` at `_variables.scss:21` is correctly *not* used as the collapse trigger (Ruling 1).
- ✅ Focus on open goes to `.modal__close` (`src/js/modal.js:14`); focus returns to the triggering card on close (`src/js/modal.js:13,20,30`); Esc closes (`:56-58`); backdrop click closes (`:44-46`); click on dialog contents does not (target check is on `modal` itself, dialog clicks don't match) (Ruling 2).
- ✅ `transitionend` hide guarded by `event.target === modal && !modal.classList.contains('modal--open')` — `src/js/modal.js:37-41` (Ruling 3).
- ✅ `body.no-scroll { overflow: hidden; }` — `src/css/_modal.scss:141-143`; modal `z-index: 200` (`_modal.scss:63`) above navbar `z-index: 100` (`src/css/_nav.scss:8`) (Ruling 4).
- ✅ `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` disables `.modal`/`.modal__dialog` transitions (`_modal.scss:134-139`); hide path without `transitionend` is handled synchronously in `closeDialog()` via `prefersReducedMotion()` check (`src/js/modal.js:28`) (Ruling 5).
- ✅ Markup untouched; selectors (`work__grid`, `work__card`, `work__role`, `work__dates`, `modal`, `modal__dialog`, `modal__close`, `modal__meta`, `modal__list`) match `src/index.html` exactly (spot-checked lines 148-297).
- ✅ Dialog `h3` gets `padding-right` clearance from the close button via `$modal-title-padding-right` — `_modal.scss:6-9, 96-99`.
- ✅ `main.js` imports and calls `initModals()` after `initCarousel()` inside the existing `DOMContentLoaded` listener — `src/js/main.js:3,8`.
- ✅ No `style=""`, `<style>`, inline `<script>`, or `<table>`; SCSS reuses `$radius`, `$color-*`, `flex-center`, `card` mixin, and the `_carousel.scss`-style pattern of naming shared geometry as variables instead of magic numbers.
- ✅ At least one CSS transition present (opacity + transform fades).
- ⚠️ Visual correctness at the five graded resolutions (1920x1080 … 1024x768) — not independently re-rendered; report's manual checks at 1280/768/600 are plausible given the CSS but not verified by me.

### Strengths

- Single `closeDialog()`/`openDialog()` pair is the only path in and out of a modal — no duplicated close logic across the close button, backdrop, and Escape handlers.
- The `transitionend` guard and reduced-motion synchronous hide are exactly the two things the brief's naive sample code (`Step 2` in the brief) got wrong, and both are correctly fixed here.
- Naming shared close-button geometry (`$modal-close-inset`, `$modal-close-size`, `$modal-close-clearance`) as derived SCSS variables instead of duplicating magic numbers between `.modal__close` and the `h3` padding is good practice and matches the `_carousel.scss` convention cited in the brief.

### Issues

#### Important

- **No protection against a second modal opening while one is already open (`src/js/modal.js:7-15`).** `openDialog()` unconditionally does `modal.hidden = false; ...; openModal = modal;` with no check for an existing `openModal`. Because all `.modal` elements sit as later siblings after the six `.work__card` triggers in the DOM (`src/index.html:148-178` vs `226+`) and `hidden` modals are removed from tab order while an *open* modal is not focus-trapped, Shift+Tab from the open modal's `.modal__close` (its only focusable descendant) lands back on a preceding `.work__card` trigger button while the current modal is still open and visible. Pressing Enter there calls `openDialog()` again: the old modal is left `hidden=false`/`modal--open` with no live reference (orphaned — its own backdrop-click listener still fires `closeDialog()`, but that function now only acts on the new `openModal`, so clicking the old modal's backdrop silently closes the *new* modal instead of itself), while `openModal`/`lastTrigger` now point only at the second modal. Net effect: two full-screen modals stacked, keyboard/backdrop control desynced from what's visually on top, and no way to close the orphaned one through documented ×/Esc/backdrop paths. This is the exact "opening a second modal while one is open" case the review was scoped to check, and it is reachable via keyboard alone (no exotic script needed).
- **Same-tick open→close race leaves a permanently stuck, unresponsive modal (`src/js/modal.js:7-15` interacting with `:17-31`).** `openDialog()` schedules `requestAnimationFrame(() => modal.classList.add('modal--open'))` but never cancels it. If `closeDialog()` runs before that rAF callback fires (e.g., Escape pressed in the same frame the modal opened, plausible for a keyboard user who activates a trigger with Enter and taps Escape immediately after, or via any near-simultaneous input), the sequence is: open() sets `hidden=false`, schedules the class-add, sets `openModal`; close() then runs, sets `openModal = null`, removes `no-scroll`, removes `modal--open` (currently absent — no-op); the previously queued rAF then fires and re-adds `modal--open`, making the modal fade back in even though the app believes nothing is open. Because `openModal` is now `null`, every subsequent close attempt (Esc, backdrop click, even a fresh `.modal__close` click if reachable) hits the `if (!openModal) return;` guard in `closeDialog()` (`:18`) and no-ops — the modal is stuck open with no code path left to hide it, and `no-scroll` has already been removed so the page behind it scrolls while a full-viewport modal sits inert on top. This directly matches the "re-open during fade-out" case flagged for review.

#### Minor

- No focus trap inside the open modal (Tab/Shift+Tab can leave the dialog to background content). Not explicitly required by Ruling 2 (which only specifies focus-on-open and focus-return-on-close), but it's the root cause enabling the Important double-open finding above; worth adding `Tab`/`Shift+Tab` wrapping within `.modal__dialog` while `openModal` is set.
- `modal.querySelector('.modal__close')` is called twice per modal (once in `openDialog` at `:14`, once in `initModals` at `:43`) — trivial, but could be cached per modal in a `Map` alongside the guard work.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Needs fixes

**Reasoning:** The rulings that were the explicit focus of this task (grid breakpoints, transitionend guard, reduced-motion hide, z-index/no-scroll, focus-on-open/return-on-close) are all correctly implemented and verifiable in the diff. However, the two open/close state-machine bugs above are real, reachable (one via ordinary keyboard navigation, the other via a same-frame open/close race) and match precisely the failure modes this review was scoped to check ("opening a second modal while one is open" and "re-open during fade-out"). Both stem from the same root cause: `openDialog`/`closeDialog` operate on a single shared `openModal`/`lastTrigger` pair with no guard against re-entrancy and no cancellation of the pending rAF class-add. These can leave the UI in an unrecoverable stuck-open or cross-modal-close state, which is a correctness gap serious enough to block approval until `openDialog` refuses/handles the already-open case (e.g., close the previous modal first, or ignore triggers while `openModal` is set) and the rAF callback is guarded or cancelled if a close happens first.

    const SHRINK_AT = 50; // px scrolled before the navbar shrinks
    const NAV_SMALL = 60; // keep in sync with $nav-height-small
    const SCROLL_DURATION = 700; // ms
    
    const navbar = () => document.getElementById('navbar');
    
    function updateNavSize() {
      navbar().classList.toggle('navbar--shrunk', window.scrollY > SHRINK_AT);
    }
    
    // Highlight the menu item for the section directly below the navbar's bottom edge
    function updateActiveLink() {
      const sections = [...document.querySelectorAll('main > section[id]')];
      const navBottom = navbar().getBoundingClientRect().bottom;
    
      let current = sections[0];
      sections.forEach((section) => {
        if (section.getBoundingClientRect().top <= navBottom + 1) current = section;
      });
    
      // The last section may be too short to reach the navbar, so force it at the bottom
      const atBottom =
        window.innerHeight + window.scrollY >= document.documentElement.scrollHeight - 2;
      if (atBottom) current = sections[sections.length - 1];
    
      document.querySelectorAll('.navbar__links .nav-link').forEach((link) => {
        link.classList.toggle('nav-link--active', link.getAttribute('href') === `#${current.id}`);
      });
    }
    
    const easeInOutCubic = (t) => (t < 0.5 ? 4 * t * t * t : 1 - (-2 * t + 2) ** 3 / 2);
    
    let scrollAnimationId = null;
    
    function smoothScrollTo(targetY) {
      if (window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches) {

    12	function updateActiveLink() {
    13	  const sections = [...document.querySelectorAll('main > section[id]')];
    14	  const navBottom = navbar().getBoundingClientRect().bottom;
    15	
    16	  let current = sections[0];
    17	  sections.forEach((section) => {
    18	    if (section.getBoundingClientRect().top <= navBottom + 1) current = section;
    19	  });
    20	
    21	  // The last section may be too short to reach the navbar, so force it at the bottom
    22	  const atBottom =
    23	    window.innerHeight + window.scrollY >= document.documentElement.scrollHeight - 2;
    24	  if (atBottom) current = sections[sections.length - 1];
    25	
    26	  document.querySelectorAll('.navbar__links .nav-link').forEach((link) => {
    27	    link.classList.toggle('nav-link--active', link.getAttribute('href') === `#${current.id}`);
    28	  });
    29	}

    1	export function initCarousel(root) {
    2	  if (!root) return;
    3	
    4	  const track = root.querySelector('.carousel__track');
    5	  const slides = [...root.querySelectorAll('.carousel__slide')];
    6	  const dotsWrap = root.querySelector('.carousel__dots');
    7	  let index = 0;
    8	
    9	  const dots = slides.map((_, i) => {
    10	    const dot = document.createElement('button');
    11	    dot.type = 'button';
    12	    dot.className = 'carousel__dot';
    13	    dot.setAttribute('aria-label', `Go to slide ${i + 1}`);
    14	    dot.addEventListener('click', () => goTo(i));
    15	    dotsWrap.appendChild(dot);
    16	    return dot;
    17	  });
    18	
    19	  function goTo(i) {
    20	    index = (i + slides.length) % slides.length; // wrap around at both ends
    21	    track.style.transform = `translateX(-${index * 100}%)`;
    22	    dots.forEach((d, j) => {
    23	      const active = j === index;
    24	      d.classList.toggle('carousel__dot--active', active);
    25	      if (active) {
    26	        d.setAttribute('aria-current', 'true');
    27	      } else {
    28	        d.removeAttribute('aria-current');
    29	      }
    30	    });
    31	  }
    32	
    33	  root.querySelector('.carousel__arrow--prev').addEventListener('click', () => goTo(index - 1));
    34	  root.querySelector('.carousel__arrow--next').addEventListener('click', () => goTo(index + 1));
    35	
    36	  root.addEventListener('keydown', (event) => {
    37	    if (event.key === 'ArrowLeft') goTo(index - 1);
    38	    if (event.key === 'ArrowRight') goTo(index + 1);
    39	  });
    40	
    41	  goTo(0);
    42	}

    4	let openModal = null;
    5	let lastTrigger = null; // the card button that opened it, so we can return focus on close
    6	let pendingFrame = null; // rAF id for the queued fade-in, so a same-frame close/switch can cancel it
    7	
    8	// Cancels any queued fade-in and snaps a modal straight to hidden, with no fade and no focus
    9	// restore. Used when a modal gets orphaned by another one opening on top of it (no visible
    10	// backdrop to click, no way to close it otherwise) — not part of the normal close path.
    11	function hideImmediately(modal) {
    12	  if (pendingFrame !== null) {
    13	    cancelAnimationFrame(pendingFrame);
    14	    pendingFrame = null;
    15	  }
    16	  modal.classList.remove('modal--open');
    17	  modal.hidden = true;
    18	}
    19	
    20	function openDialog(modal, trigger) {
    21	  // A modal was already open (e.g. Shift+Tab left it, then Enter on another card): close it
    22	  // immediately rather than leaving it visible and orphaned with no way to dismiss it.
    23	  if (openModal && openModal !== modal) hideImmediately(openModal);
    24	
    25	  modal.hidden = false;
    26	  // Add the open class on the next frame so the opacity/transform transition actually runs.
    27	  pendingFrame = requestAnimationFrame(() => {
    28	    pendingFrame = null;
    29	    modal.classList.add('modal--open');
    30	  });
    31	  document.body.classList.add('no-scroll');
    32	  openModal = modal;
    33	  lastTrigger = trigger;

Reuse variables/mixins (e.g. `card` mixin, `$radius`) instead of repeating literals.
- JS: `src/js/main.js` imports `initNav` and `initCarousel` and calls them in a `DOMContentLoaded` listener. Add `import { initModals } from './modal.js';` and call `initModals();` after `initCarousel(...)`. Match `src/js/nav.js` / `src/js/carousel.js` style.
- Markup already exists in `src/index.html` (read `#work` and the modal blocks at the end of `<body>`):
  - 6 cards: `button.work__card[data-modal-target]` each containing an `i` icon, `h3`, `p.work__role`, `p.work__dates`.
  - 6 modals: `div.modal#modal-*[role=dialog][aria-modal=true][aria-labelledby][hidden]` > `div.modal__dialog` > `button.modal__close`, `h3#…-title`, `p.modal__meta`, `ul.modal__list` of `li`.
  Style what exists: card icon (accent, large), `.work__role` (text color, semibold), `.work__dates` (muted, small); `.modal__meta` (muted, small, margin below heading), `.modal__list` (left-aligned bullets with comfortable spacing, readable line length). The `h3` in the dialog needs right padding so it doesn't run under the close button.

## Rulings (decided — follow them)
1. Work grid: 3 columns on desktop AND at 768px (768x1024 is a graded resolution; multi-column matters). Collapse to 1 column only at `respond-to(small)` (≤600px) — NOT at `mobile` as the brief's sample says. Cards should have equal height per row (grid default stretch is fine).
2. Focus management: on open, focus the close button (brief). On close, return focus to the card button that opened the modal. Esc closes; backdrop click closes; clicks inside the dialog don't.
3. The fade-out: the brief hides the modal on `transitionend`. Guard it so a `transitionend` bubbling from a child (e.g. the dialog's transform transition) or a fast re-open can't hide an open modal: only hide if the event target is the modal itself AND the modal is not currently open. Keep the logic small.
4. `body.no-scroll { overflow: hidden; }` as in the brief is fine. The fixed navbar has z-index 100; the modal must sit above it (brief's 200 is fine).
5. Respect `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` by disabling the modal's opacity/transform transitions — BUT then `transitionend` never fires, so hiding must still happen (e.g. hide immediately when reduced motion is preferred, or when the computed transition duration is 0). Keep it simple and correct.

    import { initNav } from './nav.js';
    import { initCarousel } from './carousel.js';
    
    document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', () => {
      initNav();
      initCarousel(document.getElementById('carousel'));

    --- nav.js ---
    const SHRINK_AT = 50; // px scrolled before the navbar shrinks
    const NAV_SMALL = 60; // keep in sync with $nav-height-small
    const SCROLL_DURATION = 700; // ms
    
    const navbar = () => document.getElementById('navbar');
    
    function updateNavSize() {
      navbar().classList.toggle('navbar--shrunk', window.scrollY > SHRINK_AT);
    }
    
    // Highlight the menu item for the section directly below the navbar's bottom edge
    function updateActiveLink() {
      const sections = [...document.querySelectorAll('main > section[id]')];
      const navBottom = navbar().getBoundingClientRect().bottom;
    
      let current = sections[0];
      sections.forEach((section) => {
        if (section.getBoundingClientRect().top <= navBottom + 1) current = section;
      });
    
      // The last section may be too short to reach the navbar, so force it at the bottom
      const atBottom =
        window.innerHeight + window.scrollY >= document.documentElement.scrollHeight - 2;
      if (atBottom) current = sections[sections.length - 1];
    
      document.querySelectorAll('.navbar__links .nav-link').forEach((link) => {
        link.classList.toggle('nav-link--active', link.getAttribute('href') === `#${current.id}`);
      });
    }
    
    const easeInOutCubic = (t) => (t < 0.5 ? 4 * t * t * t : 1 - (-2 * t + 2) ** 3 / 2);

    ... [2505 more characters truncated]

----- CLAUDE (2026-09-23 00:34:24) -----
Found an edge case: canceling the rAF prevents the fade-in, but then `closeDialog` never sets `hidden = true` (no transition ever started, so `transitionend` never fires) — the modal is left invisible but not `hidden`, blocking the page. Fixing this.
  [tool call: Read]
    {
     "file_path": "/Users/darshan/Documents/Fall 2026/CS-409-Art of web programming/MP1/mp1/src/js/modal.js"
    }
  [tool result]
    1	const prefersReducedMotion = () =>
    2	  window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches;
    3	
    4	let openModal = null;
    5	let lastTrigger = null; // the card button that opened it, so we can return focus on close
    6	let pendingFrame = null; // rAF id for the queued fade-in, so a same-frame close/switch can cancel it
    7	
    8	// Cancels any queued fade-in and snaps a modal straight to hidden, with no fade and no focus
    9	// restore. Used when a modal gets orphaned by another one opening on top of it (no visible
    10	// backdrop to click, no way to close it otherwise) — not part of the normal close path.
    11	function hideImmediately(modal) {
    12	  if (pendingFrame !== null) {
    13	    cancelAnimationFrame(pendingFrame);
    14	    pendingFrame = null;
    15	  }
    16	  modal.classList.remove('modal--open');
    17	  modal.hidden = true;
    18	}
    19	
    20	function openDialog(modal, trigger) {
    21	  // A modal was already open (e.g. Shift+Tab left it, then Enter on another card): close it
    22	  // immediately rather than leaving it visible and orphaned with no way to dismiss it.
    23	  if (openModal && openModal !== modal) hideImmediately(openModal);
    24	
    25	  modal.hidden = false;
    26	  // Add the open class on the next frame so the opacity/transform transition actually runs.
    27	  pendingFrame = requestAnimationFrame(() => {
    28	    pendingFrame = null;
    29	    modal.classList.add('modal--open');
    30	  });
    31	  document.body.classList.add('no-scroll');
    32	  openModal = modal;
    33	  lastTrigger = trigger;
    34

----- CLAUDE (2026-09-23 00:23:02) -----
Good, confirms `.tags` base rule exists and carousel only adds `margin-top`. Now I have enough to write the review.

### Spec Compliance

**src/css/_carousel.scss**
- Matches ruling 1: `.carousel__icon` and `.carousel__subtitle` are styled, no `img` rule present (correctly dropped vs. the brief's stub which had `img { ... }`).
- Matches ruling 2: slide padding `3rem 4.5rem` desktop, stepped down at `respond-to(tablet)` (2.5rem 3rem) and `respond-to(mobile)` (2rem 1.5rem); uses `$color-surface` and `$radius` (line 26, 37-38). Improves on the brief's stub, which hardcoded `12px` for `&__viewport { border-radius: 12px; }` — implementer correctly used `$radius` instead (line 25).
- Matches ruling 3: arrows at `-24px` desktop, `8px` inside `respond-to(tablet)` (lines 98-113) — no separate mobile override, which is fine since tablet's rule cascades down to mobile (`respond-to` uses `max-width`, so mobile also matches tablet's query).
- ⚠️ Centering claim ("vertically centered on the card, not on card+dots") is only approximated — see Issues (Important) below; not fully correct by my calculation.
- Matches ruling 6 (SCSS variables/mixins reuse): uses `$color-surface`, `$color-muted`, `$color-accent`, `$color-bg`, `$color-text`, `$radius`, `respond-to`, `flex-center` — all confirmed present in `_variables.scss`/`_mixins.scss`. No unused/undefined tokens.
- Nothing extra beyond what's needed; `.tags` override is a single `margin-top`, correctly deferring to the base `.tags` rule in `_base.scss:84` rather than duplicating it.

**src/js/carousel.js**
- Matches ruling 4: `ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight` listener is attached to `root` (carousel container), not `document` (line 190 in diff / carousel.js), so it only fires via bubbling when focus is inside the carousel. Correct.
- Matches ruling 3: only `track.style.transform` is set from JS (line 175). No other inline styles.
- Matches ruling 5: active dot gets both `carousel__dot--active` class and `aria-current="true"`, removed from others (lines 176-184) — this is an improvement over the brief's stub, which only toggled the class.
- `initCarousel(root)` signature matches brief exactly.
- No autoplay, no swipe — confirmed, nothing beyond arrows/dots/keyboard.
- ⚠️ No `preventDefault()` on the arrow-key handler — brief doesn't explicitly require it, so not a compliance miss, but worth noting under code quality.

**src/js/main.js**
- Matches ruling exactly: `import { initCarousel } from './carousel.js'` added, `initCarousel(document.getElementById('carousel'))` called immediately after `initNav()` inside the existing `DOMContentLoaded` listener (diff lines 142-148). No leftover or misordered code.

Markup cross-check (src/index.html:78-140): confirms the report's claims — 4 `li.carousel__slide`, each with `i.carousel__icon`, `h3`, `p.carousel__subtitle`, description `p`, `ul.tags`; arrows are direct children of `.carousel` (siblings of `.carousel__viewport`), so the `-24px` desktop overhang is not clipped by the viewport's `overflow: hidden` (viewport only wraps the track, not the arrows). HTML was untouched, as claimed and as required.

### Strengths
- Correctly diverged from the brief's stub in two places where the ruling supersedes it: dropped the `img` rule (no images in markup) and used `$radius` instead of a hardcoded `12px`.
- `aria-current="true"` addition on the active dot is a genuine accessibility improvement beyond the brief's minimum.
- Keyboard handling correctly scoped to the carousel root via bubbling rather than a document-level listener — satisfies ruling 4 precisely.
- Clean reuse of `.tags` base styling; no duplicated CSS.
- Wrap-around index math `(i + slides.length) % slides.length` is correct for single-step increments/decrements from any valid `index` (verified below).

### Issues

#### Important
- **`src/css/_carousel.scss:79-81`** — Arrow vertical centering overshoots. `top: calc(50% - 1.75rem)` plus `transform: translateY(-50%)` is meant to shift the arrow's center up from the midpoint of `.carousel` (card + dots) to the midpoint of the card alone, per ruling 2. The needed shift is half the height added by the dots block below the viewport: dots row (~10px, from `.carousel__dot` width/height) + `margin-top: 1rem` (16px) ≈ 26px total, so the correct offset is ≈13px (0.8125rem). The implementation uses 28px (1.75rem) — more than double what's needed — so the arrows will sit visibly too high, roughly 15px above the true vertical center of the card, at every breakpoint (this rule isn't inside any `respond-to` block, so it applies universally). This directly fails the specific ruling that arrows be "vertically centered on the card, not on card+dots."

#### Minor
- **`src/js/carousel.js` (arrow-key handler, ~lines 190-193)** — No `event.preventDefault()` on `ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight`. Not required by the brief, but if focus ever lands on a scrollable ancestor this could double up with native scroll behavior. Low risk since focus stays on buttons, but cheap to add for robustness.
- **`src/css/_carousel.scss:78-114`** — `.carousel__arrow` buttons don't use the `flex-center` mixin (or any explicit `display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center`) to center the Font Awesome chevron icon inside the 48×48 circle. It's likely close enough visually given default button text-align/UA styling, but this is inconsistent with `.carousel__dots` (which does use `flex-center`) and leaves icon centering to browser defaults rather than explicit CSS — a DRY/consistency nit rather than a functional bug.
- Report's verification section (task-8-report.md) explicitly states no live-DOM/browser check was performed — the vertical-centering issue above is exactly the kind of defect a quick visual check would have caught. Not a spec miss per se, but the "Verification" claim of confidence is weaker than it reads.

### Assessment

**Task quality:** Needs fixes

**Reasoning:** All three files are otherwise faithful to the brief and the controller rulings — markup/selectors line up exactly, the JS logic (wrap-around math, dot sync, `aria-current`, keyboard scoping, no autoplay/swipe) is correct and clean, and `main.js` wiring is exact. However, ruling 2 explicitly calls for arrows "vertically centered on the card, not on card+dots," and the chosen offset (`1.75rem`) overshoots the correct value (~`0.8125rem`) by roughly 2x, so the arrows will be visibly misaligned relative to the card at every resolution. That's a concrete, calculable defect against an explicit ruling, not a matter of taste, so this task can't be trusted as complete until the offset is corrected (or centering is redone, e.g. by wrapping viewport+arrows in their own positioning container so `top: 50%` needs no manual compensation at all).

    ## README.md (full new content; old content was the course assignment text)
    # Darshan Dhene — Portfolio (CS 409 MP1)
    
    A single-page personal portfolio built with plain HTML, SCSS and vanilla ES6 (no UI/JS frameworks or libraries). It presents an about section, a project carousel, a work-experience grid with detail modals, an embedded video and contact/social links, all on one scrolling page with a sticky, resizing navbar and scroll-position highlighting.
    
    - **Live site:** https://darshandhene.github.io/mp1/
    - **Repository:** https://github.com/darshandhene/mp1
    
    ## Run locally
    
    ```bash
    npm install
    npm start      # dev server at http://localhost:8080
    npm run build  # production bundle in build/
    ```

    const SHRINK_AT = 50; // px scrolled before the navbar shrinks
    const NAV_SMALL = 60; // keep in sync with $nav-height-small
    const SCROLL_DURATION = 700; // ms
    
    const navbar = () => document.getElementById('navbar');
    
    function updateNavSize() {
      navbar().classList.toggle('navbar--shrunk', window.scrollY > SHRINK_AT);
    }
    
    // Highlight the menu item for the section directly below the navbar's bottom edge
    function updateActiveLink() {
      const sections = [...document.querySelectorAll('main > section[id]')];
      const navBottom = navbar().getBoundingClientRect().bottom;
    
      let current = sections[0];
      sections.forEach((section) => {
        if (section.getBoundingClientRect().top <= navBottom + 1) current = section;
      });
    
      // The last section may be too short to reach the navbar, so force it at the bottom
      const atBottom =
        window.innerHeight + window.scrollY >= document.documentElement.scrollHeight - 2;
      if (atBottom) current = sections[sections.length - 1];
    
      document.querySelectorAll('.navbar__links .nav-link').forEach((link) => {
        link.classList.toggle('nav-link--active', link.getAttribute('href') === `#${current.id

  [tool result]
    export function initCarousel(root) {
      if (!root) return;
    
      const track = root.querySelector('.carousel__track');
      const slides = [...root.querySelectorAll('.carousel__slide')];
      const dotsWrap = root.querySelector('.carousel__dots');
      let index = 0;
    
      const dots = slides.map((_, i) => {
        const dot = document.createElement('button');
        dot.type = 'button';
        dot.className = 'carousel__dot';
        dot.setAttribute('aria-label', `Go to slide ${i + 1}`);
        dot.addEventListener('click', () => goTo(i));
        dotsWrap.appendChild(dot);
        return dot;
      });
    
      function goTo(i) {
        index = (i + slides.length) % slides.length; // wrap around at both ends
        track.style.transform = `translateX(-${index * 100}%)`;
        dots.forEach((d, j) => {
          const active = j === index;
          d.classList.toggle('carousel__dot--active', active);
          if (active) {
            d.setAttribute('aria-current', 'true');
          } else {
            d.removeAttribute('aria-current');
          }
        });
      }
    
      root.querySelector('.carousel__arrow--prev').addEventListener('click', () => goTo(index - 1));
      root.querySelector('.carousel__arrow--next').addEventListener('click', () => goTo(index + 1));
    
      root.addEventListener('keydown', (event) => {
        if (event.key === 'ArrowLeft') goTo(index - 1);
        if (event.key === 'ArrowRight') goTo(index + 1);
      });
    
      goTo(0);
    }

    const prefersReducedMotion = () =>
      window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches;
    
    let openModal = null;
    let lastTrigger = null; // the card button that opened it, so we can return focus on close
    let pendingFrame = null; // rAF id for the queued fade-in, so a same-frame close/switch can cancel it
    
    // Cancels any queued fade-in and snaps a modal straight to hidden, with no fade and no focus
    // restore. Used when a modal gets orphaned by another one opening on top of it (no visible
    // backdrop to click, no way to close it otherwise) — not part of the normal close path.
    function hideImmediately(modal) {
      if (pendingFrame !== null) {
        cancelAnimationFrame(pendingFrame);
        pendingFrame = null;
      }
      modal.classList.remove('modal--open');
      modal.hidden = true;
    }
    
    function openDialog(modal, trigger) {
      // A modal was already open (e.g. Shift+Tab left it, then Enter on another card): close it
      // immediately rather than leaving it visible and orphaned with no way to dismiss it.
      if (openModal && openModal !== modal) hideImmediately(openModal);
    
      modal.hidden = false;
      // Add the open class on the next frame so the opacity/transform transition actually runs.
      pendingFrame = requestAnimationFrame(() => {
        pendingFrame = null;
        modal.classList.add('modal--open');
      });
      document.body.classList.add('no-scroll');
      openModal = modal;
      lastTrigger = trigger;
      modal.querySelector('.modal__close').focus();
    }
    
    function closeDialog() {
      if (!openModal)

    # ARIA Dialog Role - Page Status
    
    **The page exists and is fully functional.** It provides comprehensive documentation of the ARIA `dialog` role.
    
    ## Key Content Overview
    
    The page describes the `dialog` role as a mechanism to mark up HTML-based application dialogs or windows that:
    - Separate content/UI from the rest of the web application
    - Are typically placed on top of page content using an overlay
    - Can be either **modal** (only dialog content is interactive) or **non-modal** (content outside is still interactive)
    
    ## Core Requirements Documented
    
    The page emphasizes two critical requirements for accessible dialogs:
    
    1. **Labeling** - Use `aria-labelledby` and `aria-describedby`:
    ```html
    <div
      role="dialog"
      aria-labelledby="dialog1Title"
      aria-describedby="dialog1Desc">
      <h2 id="dialog1Title">Your personal details were successfully updated</h2>
      <p id="dialog1Desc">You can change your details at any time in the user account section.</p>
      <button>Close</button>
    </div>
    ```
    
    2. **Keyboard Focus Management** - Including focus wrapping, default focus placement, and focus restoration after dismissal
    
    ## Associated Resources
    
    The page also links to:
    - The related [`alertdialog` role](/en-US/docs/Web/Accessibility/ARIA/Reference/Roles/alertdialog_role)
    - The HTML [`<dialog>` element](/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Elements/dialog)
    - WAI-ARIA specifications

- SCSS lives in `src/css/` (not src/scss). `main.scss` already `@use`s `carousel`; `src/css/_carousel.scss` is a one-line stub — replace it. Read `_variables.scss` (colors, `$radius`, `$breakpoints` with tablet 1024px / mobile 768px / small 600px) and `_mixins.scss` (`respond-to`, `flex-center`, `card`) first and use them rather than repeating literals. `_base.scss` already styles `.tags`.
- JS: `src/js/main.js` currently imports `initNav` from './nav.js' and calls it inside a `DOMContentLoaded` listener. Add `import { initCarousel } from './carousel.js';` and call `initCarousel(document.getElementById('carousel'));` in the same listener after `initNav()`. Match `src/js/nav.js` style (2-space indent, single quotes, semicolons, named export).
- The markup already exists in `src/index.html` (read the `#projects` section): 4 slides, each `li.carousel__slide` contains `i.carousel__icon` (Font Awesome icon), `h3`, `p.carousel__subtitle`, a description `p`, and `ul.tags`. There are NO images — the brief's `img` rules don't apply; instead style `.carousel__icon` (large, accent color, margin below) and `.carousel__subtitle` (accent or muted, smaller, italic or semibold — your choice, keep it tasteful). Slides are text cards: give each slide comfortable padding (around 3rem 4.5rem on desktop so text clears the arrows, smaller at tablet/mobile) on `$color-surface` with `$radius`.
- The empty `div.carousel__dots` is filled by JS (per brief).

## Rulings (decided — follow them)
1. Slides may differ in text length; the track is a flex row, so all slides get the height of the tallest — that's fine and prevents layout jumping. Do not animate height.
2. Arrow placement: on desktop the arrows sit half outside the card (brief's -24px). At `respond-to(tablet)` (≤1024px) move them inside (8px) as the brief says. Vertically center arrows on the card: use `top: 50%` with `translateY(-50%)` relative to the carousel viewport area — but note the dots row sits below the viewport inside `.carousel`, so either wrap nothing new and compute `top` as `calc(50% - <half dots-row height>)`, or position the arrows relative to the viewport. Pick the simplest approach that stays centered on the card; do NOT change the HTML structure.
3. `track.style.transform = ...` set from JS is allowed (the no-inline-style rule is about `style=""` attributes in HTML).
4. Keyboard: arrows are `<button>`s so they're focusable already; add ArrowLeft/ArrowRight key handling ONLY when focus is inside the carousel (listen on the carousel root). Nothing else extra (no autoplay, no swipe).
5. Mark the active dot with `aria-current="true"` in addition to the class, and hidden slides don't need aria changes.

## Hard rules
- **Never run git add/commit/push/stash/checkout or anything that changes git state.** The user commits by hand.
- No `style=""` attributes / `<style>` blocks / inline `<script>` / `<table>` in HTML. No libraries. Do not edit `src/index.html`.
- Only touch: src/css/_carousel.scss, src/js/carousel.js (new), src/js/main.js.
- Do not dispatch subagents.

## Findings to fix (controller rulings — follow them exactly)
1. **Invalid HTML (Important).** `src/index.html`: the 6 `button.work__card` elements contain `<h3>` and `<p>` (flow content), which is invalid inside `<button>`. Replace them with phrasing elements: `<h3>X</h3>` → `<span class="work__title">X</span>`; `<p class="work__role">` → `<span class="work__role">`; `<p class="work__dates">` → `<span class="work__dates">`. Keep the icon `<i>` and the existing `<span class="work__cta">…</span>`. In `src/css/_modal.scss`, make `.work__title`, `.work__role`, `.work__dates` `display: block`, and move the current `.work__card h3` rules to `.work__title`, adding the heading look it loses by not being an h3 (`font-family: $font-heading`, weight 600, size ≈1.17rem, line-height like h3 in _base.scss). The result must look the same as before. Check that the `.work__cta i` vs `.work__card i` icon-size cascade still holds.
2. **Social icons hover no-op (Minor).** `src/css/_footer.scss`: `.social-icons a` is inline so its hover `transform` does nothing — add `display: inline-block`.
3. **Dead code (Minor).**
   - Delete `src/assets/image.jpg` (unused template image; confirm first with `grep -rn "image.jpg" src/` that nothing references it).
   - `src/css/_variables.scss` defines CSS custom properties `--accent` and `--nav-height` in `:root` that nothing uses. KEEP them (the assignment grades "SCSS features/CSS variables") and put them to real use: use `var(--accent)` for the `:focus-visible` outline color(s) (e.g. `.work__card:focus-visible` in _modal.scss and any other focus-visible outline) and add a `::selection` rule in `_base.scss` using `var(--accent)` for the background with `$color-bg`/white text; use `var(--nav-height)` for the hero's top padding in `_hero.scss` instead of `$nav-height-large` (only if the value is identical — it is: `--nav-height: #{$nav-height-large}`). Do not add other custom properties.
4. **Smooth scroll robustness (Minor, but smooth scrolling is 10% of the grade).** `src/js/nav.js` `smoothScrollTo`: (a) keep the current animation's rAF id in a module-level variable and `cancelAnimationFrame` it when a new scroll starts, so rapid clicks don't run two loops that fight; (b) if `window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches`, jump instantly with `window.scrollTo(0, targetY)` and return. Keep everything else (easing, duration, NAV_SMALL offset) unchanged.
5. **README wording (Minor).** `README.md`: it says "production bundle" but `webpack.config.js` builds with `mode: "development"` (course template default — do NOT change the config). Fix the wording (e.g. "build the static site into `build/`"). Leave the `<!-- TODO(user): confirm the video source -->` comment in place — the user will resolve it. If the README's project-structure or requirement table mentions `image.jpg`, `<h3>` in work cards, or the custom properties, update those lines to stay accurate.

    2	const NAV_SMALL = 60; // keep in sync with $nav-height-small
    3	const SCROLL_DURATION = 700; // ms
    4	
    5	const navbar = () => document.getElementById('navbar');
    6	
    7	function updateNavSize() {
    8	  navbar().classList.toggle('navbar--shrunk', window.scrollY > SHRINK_AT);
    9	}
    10	
    11	// Highlight the menu item for the section directly below the navbar's bottom edge
    12	function updateActiveLink() {
    13	  const sections = [...document.querySelectorAll('main > section[id]')];
    14	  const navBottom = navbar().getBoundingClientRect().bottom;
    15	
    16	  let current = sections[0];
    17	  sections.forEach((section) => {
    18	    if (section.getBoundingClientRect().top <= navBottom + 1) current = section;
    19	  });
    20	
    21	  // The last section may be too short to reach the navbar, so force it at the bottom
    22	  const atBottom =
    23	    window.innerHeight + window.scrollY >= document.documentElement.scrollHeight - 2;
    24	  if (atBottom) current = sections[sections.length - 1];
    25	
    26	  document.querySelectorAll('.navbar__links .nav-link').forEach((link) => {
    27	    link.classList.toggle('nav-link--active', link.getAttribute('href') === `#${current.id}`);
    28	  });
    29	}
